## Source: `S5_3_P2\paper_first_notes\S5_3_P2_01_WebPilot_A_Versatile_and_Autonomous_Multi-Agent_System_for_Web_Task_Execution_with_Strategic_Exploration.md`

# S5.3 P2 Paper 01 — WebPilot: A Versatile and Autonomous Multi-Agent System for Web Task Execution with Strategic Exploration

## Metadata

- **Title:** WebPilot: A Versatile and Autonomous Multi-Agent System for Web Task Execution with Strategic Exploration
- **Year:** 2024/2025
- **Verified venue/status:** AAAI 2025
- **Peer-reviewed status:** Yes
- **Thesis section:** S5.3 — Planning, Search, Memory, and Long-Horizon Decision-Making for LLM Agents
- **Main category:** strategic exploration / multi-agent web task execution
- **S5.3 role:** search-based and exploration-based planning
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Strong citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Medium to high
- **BibTeX key:** `webpilotversatileautonomousmulti2024`

---

## Simple understanding

WebPilot: A Versatile and Autonomous Multi-Agent System for Web Task Execution with Strategic Exploration studies search or exploration for agents. It is important because web and GUI agents often fail when they commit too early to one path without exploring alternatives.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Web/GUI/computer-use agents fail when they act reactively, forget context, cannot recover, or explore inefficiently.
Paper → This work adds planning, search, memory, reflection, world modeling, skill reuse, or context management.
Goal → Make the agent more reliable on long-horizon interactive tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the paper changes inside the agent decision loop**.  
Only after that should it be connected to S5.3 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Improve agent planning by exploring multiple possible action trajectories and selecting more promising paths using search, reflection, or value estimates.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
strategic exploration / multi-agent web task execution
```

This belongs to the agent planning layer:

```text
task instruction
→ current observation/state
→ planning or search
→ candidate actions
→ evaluation/reflection/correction
→ execution
→ memory/context update
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Search-based or exploration-based planning can improve long-horizon task success, but it usually increases compute cost and requires reliable evaluation signals.

For S5.3, the important question is:

```text
How does the method improve decision-making beyond one-step ReAct-style action selection?
```

Typical S5.3 improvements include:

- planning ahead,
- exploring multiple branches,
- reflecting on failure,
- backtracking after mistakes,
- using memory and replay,
- summarizing long contexts,
- learning reusable skills,
- simulating action consequences,
- and allocating interaction budget more intelligently.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **Search/exploration algorithm design.**
- **Benchmark success-rate improvement over reactive baselines.**
- **Ablations showing the effect of search, reflection, or evaluation components.**

Also extract, if available:

- planning/search algorithm,
- memory representation,
- action space,
- benchmark and task horizon,
- success-rate improvement,
- compute/cost trade-off,
- ablation study,
- and failure modes.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** Search increases latency and token/tool cost.
- **Limitation 2:** Value estimation or reflection can be noisy.
- **Limitation 3:** Search over web/GUI environments can become expensive because actions change external state.

General thesis-level limitation:

```text
Better planning does not automatically mean generalized web automation is solved.
The agent still needs perception, grounding, execution, verification, recovery, and safety.
```

Therefore, this paper should be used as **P2 support**, not as the only foundation for S5.3.

---

## Venue/status caution

This is safe for stronger thesis claims because the verified venue/status is **AAAI 2025**.

For final thesis writing:

```text
peer-reviewed main conference/journal → stronger citation
system/demo paper → useful architecture/tooling citation
arXiv/technical report/submission → recent trend; cite cautiously
```

---

## Relation to S5.3

This paper belongs in **S5.3** because S5.3 discusses the planning and decision-making layer of LLM-based agents.

Its role is:

```text
WebPilot: A Versatile and Autonomous Multi-Agent System for Web Task Execution with Strategic Exploration
→ search-based and exploration-based planning
→ P2 support for planning/search/memory/long-horizon decision-making
```

Use it after explaining its mechanism.  
Do not introduce it only as “P2”; introduce the planning problem first.

---

## Relation to the thesis topic

Your thesis topic is:

```text
LLM-based agents for generalized web automation and data extraction
```

This paper matters because generalized web automation requires agents that can handle multi-step and uncertain web tasks.

For web automation and data extraction, planning is needed for:

```text
finding target pages
navigating multi-page sites
avoiding irrelevant branches
recovering from wrong clicks
remembering previous attempts
using tools or skills
verifying whether extraction is complete
```

This paper supports that pipeline by improving:

```text
search-based and exploration-based planning
```

---

- Use it as **P2 support** in S5.3.

- Extract one limitation or failure mode.

---

## Simple understanding

R2D2: Remembering, Replaying and Dynamic Decision Making with a Reflective Agentic Memory focuses on memory or context management for agents. It matters because long-horizon agents must remember goals, past attempts, errors, and useful procedures without overloading the context window.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Web/GUI/computer-use agents fail when they act reactively, forget context, cannot recover, or explore inefficiently.
Paper → This work adds planning, search, memory, reflection, world modeling, skill reuse, or context management.

```

The first goal is to understand **what the paper changes inside the agent decision loop**.

## Core idea

Use memory, replay, summarization, or program-guided context to preserve relevant information during long-horizon interaction.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
reflective agentic memory / replay / dynamic decision-making
```

This belongs to the agent planning layer:

```text
task instruction
→ current observation/state
→ planning or search
→ candidate actions
→ evaluation/reflection/correction
→ execution
→ memory/context update
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Memory/context mechanisms can reduce repeated mistakes and help agents maintain task progress, but they can also introduce stale or compressed information errors.

For S5.3, the important question is:

```text
How does the method improve decision-making beyond one-step ReAct-style action selection?
```

Typical S5.3 improvements include:

- planning ahead,

- **Recommended citation strength:** Strong citation

---

## Simple understanding

PlanGenLLMs: A Modern Survey of LLM Planning Capabilities is a survey or position-style paper about planning, reasoning, memory, or agent efficiency. Its main value is taxonomy and synthesis rather than a new agent algorithm.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Web/GUI/computer-use agents fail when they act reactively, forget context, cannot recover, or explore inefficiently.
Paper → This work adds planning, search, memory, reflection, world modeling, skill reuse, or context management.
Goal → Make the agent more reliable on long-horizon interactive tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the paper changes inside the agent decision loop**.  
Only after that should it be connected to S5.3 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Organize the literature around planning/reasoning/memory/tool-learning methods for LLM-based agents.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
survey of LLM planning capabilities
```

This belongs to the agent planning layer:

```text
task instruction
→ current observation/state
→ planning or search
→ candidate actions
→ evaluation/reflection/correction
→ execution
→ memory/context update
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Agent planning is not a single technique: it includes decomposition, search, reflection, memory, tool use, context management, and evaluation.

For S5.3, the important question is:

```text
How does the method improve decision-making beyond one-step ReAct-style action selection?
```

Typical S5.3 improvements include:

- planning ahead,
- exploring multiple branches,
- reflecting on failure,
- backtracking after mistakes,
- using memory and replay,
- summarizing long contexts,
- learning reusable skills,
- simulating action consequences,
- and allocating interaction budget more intelligently.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- ablation study,
- and failure modes.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** Survey papers are secondary sources and should not replace primary method papers.
- **Limitation 2:** Fast-moving agent research can make survey taxonomies outdated quickly.
- **Limitation 3:** The paper may be broad and not focused only on web automation.

General thesis-level limitation:

```text

The agent still needs perception, grounding, execution, verification, recovery, and safety.
```

Therefore, this paper should be used as **P2 support**, not as the only foundation for S5.3.

# S5.3 P2 Paper 09 — InfiGUI-R1: Advancing Multimodal GUI Agents from Reactive Actors to Deliberative Reasoners

## Metadata

- **Title:** InfiGUI-R1: Advancing Multimodal GUI Agents from Reactive Actors to Deliberative Reasoners
- **Year:** 2025
- **Verified venue/status:** arXiv 2504.14239 / OpenReview page
- **Peer-reviewed status:** No confirmed accepted venue
- **Thesis section:** S5.3 — Planning, Search, Memory, and Long-Horizon Decision-Making for LLM Agents
- **Main category:** deliberative reasoning for multimodal GUI agents
- **S5.3 role:** planning and decision-making for LLM agents
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Support/recent-trend citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Low to medium
- **BibTeX key:** `infiguir1advancingmultimodal2025`

---

## Simple understanding

InfiGUI-R1: Advancing Multimodal GUI Agents from Reactive Actors to Deliberative Reasoners supports S5.3 by addressing planning, decision-making, memory, exploration, or long-horizon control in LLM-based agents.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Web/GUI/computer-use agents fail when they act reactively, forget context, cannot recover, or explore inefficiently.
Paper → This work adds planning, search, memory, reflection, world modeling, skill reuse, or context management.
Goal → Make the agent more reliable on long-horizon interactive tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the paper changes inside the agent decision loop**.  
Only after that should it be connected to S5.3 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Improve the agent planning loop beyond one-step reactive action selection.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
deliberative reasoning for multimodal GUI agents
```

This belongs to the agent planning layer:

```text
task instruction
→ current observation/state
→ planning or search
→ candidate actions
→ evaluation/reflection/correction
→ execution
→ memory/context update
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Long-horizon agents need more structured planning and feedback than standard ReAct-style interaction.

For S5.3, the important question is:

```text
How does the method improve decision-making beyond one-step ReAct-style action selection?
```

Typical S5.3 improvements include:

- planning ahead,
- exploring multiple branches,
- reflecting on failure,
- backtracking after mistakes,
- using memory and replay,
- summarizing long contexts,
- learning reusable skills,
- simulating action consequences,
- and allocating interaction budget more intelligently.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **Planning architecture.**
- **Benchmark results.**
- **Ablation or failure analysis.**

Also extract, if available:

- planning/search algorithm,
- memory representation,
- action space,
- benchmark and task horizon,
- success-rate improvement,
- compute/cost trade-off,
- ablation study,
- and failure modes.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** The method may be benchmark-specific.
- **Limitation 2:** Planning improvements can increase compute cost.
- **Limitation 3:** Success in controlled environments may not transfer fully to live web automation.

General thesis-level limitation:

```text
Better planning does not automatically mean generalized web automation is solved.
The agent still needs perception, grounding, execution, verification, recovery, and safety.
```

Therefore, this paper should be used as **P2 support**, not as the only foundation for S5.3.

- **Year:** 2025

- **Peer-reviewed status:** No confirmed accepted venue
- **Thesis section:** S5.3 — Planning, Search, Memory, and Long-Horizon Decision-Making for LLM Agents

- **Recommended reading depth:** Low to medium
- **BibTeX key:** `thinkingvsdoingthat2025`

---

## Simple understanding

Thinking vs. Doing: Agents that Reason by Scaling Test-Time Interaction supports S5.3 by addressing planning, decision-making, memory, exploration, or long-horizon control in LLM-based agents.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Web/GUI/computer-use agents fail when they act reactively, forget context, cannot recover, or explore inefficiently.
Paper → This work adds planning, search, memory, reflection, world modeling, skill reuse, or context management.
Goal → Make the agent more reliable on long-horizon interactive tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the paper changes inside the agent decision loop**.  
Only after that should it be connected to S5.3 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Improve the agent planning loop beyond one-step reactive action selection.

```text
test-time interaction scaling
```

This belongs to the agent planning layer:

```text

→ planning or search

→ evaluation/reflection/correction
→ execution
```

## Key finding / main claim

Long-horizon agents need more structured planning and feedback than standard ReAct-style interaction.

For S5.3, the important question is:

```text
How does the method improve decision-making beyond one-step ReAct-style action selection?
```

Typical S5.3 improvements include:

- planning ahead,
- exploring multiple branches,
- reflecting on failure,
- backtracking after mistakes,
- using memory and replay,
- summarizing long contexts,
- learning reusable skills,
- simulating action consequences,
- and allocating interaction budget more intelligently.

# S5.3 P2 Paper 15 — ReSum: Unlocking Long-Horizon Search Intelligence via Context Summarization

## Metadata

- **Title:** ReSum: Unlocking Long-Horizon Search Intelligence via Context Summarization
- **Year:** 2025
- **Verified venue/status:** arXiv 2509.13313 / OpenReview page
- **Peer-reviewed status:** No confirmed accepted venue
- **Thesis section:** S5.3 — Planning, Search, Memory, and Long-Horizon Decision-Making for LLM Agents
- **Main category:** context summarization / long-horizon search intelligence
- **S5.3 role:** search-based and exploration-based planning
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Support/recent-trend citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Low to medium
- **BibTeX key:** `resumunlockinglonghorizon2025`

---

## Simple understanding

ReSum: Unlocking Long-Horizon Search Intelligence via Context Summarization studies search or exploration for agents. It is important because web and GUI agents often fail when they commit too early to one path without exploring alternatives.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Web/GUI/computer-use agents fail when they act reactively, forget context, cannot recover, or explore inefficiently.
Paper → This work adds planning, search, memory, reflection, world modeling, skill reuse, or context management.
Goal → Make the agent more reliable on long-horizon interactive tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the paper changes inside the agent decision loop**.  
Only after that should it be connected to S5.3 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Improve agent planning by exploring multiple possible action trajectories and selecting more promising paths using search, reflection, or value estimates.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
context summarization / long-horizon search intelligence
```

This belongs to the agent planning layer:

```text
task instruction
→ current observation/state
→ planning or search
→ candidate actions
→ evaluation/reflection/correction
→ execution
→ memory/context update
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Search-based or exploration-based planning can improve long-horizon task success, but it usually increases compute cost and requires reliable evaluation signals.

For S5.3, the important question is:

```text
How does the method improve decision-making beyond one-step ReAct-style action selection?
```

Typical S5.3 improvements include:

- planning ahead,
- exploring multiple branches,
- reflecting on failure,
- backtracking after mistakes,
- using memory and replay,
- summarizing long contexts,
- learning reusable skills,
- simulating action consequences,
- and allocating interaction budget more intelligently.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **Search/exploration algorithm design.**

- **Ablations showing the effect of search, reflection, or evaluation components.**

Also extract, if available:

- planning/search algorithm,
- memory representation,
- action space,
- benchmark and task horizon,
- success-rate improvement,
- compute/cost trade-off,
- ablation study,
- and failure modes.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** Search increases latency and token/tool cost.
- **Limitation 2:** Value estimation or reflection can be noisy.
- **Limitation 3:** Search over web/GUI environments can become expensive because actions change external state.

General thesis-level limitation:

```text
Better planning does not automatically mean generalized web automation is solved.
The agent still needs perception, grounding, execution, verification, recovery, and safety.
```

# S5.3 P2 Paper 17 — Toward Efficient Agents: Memory, Tool Learning, and Planning

## Metadata

- **Title:** Toward Efficient Agents: Memory, Tool Learning, and Planning
- **Year:** 2026
- **Verified venue/status:** arXiv / survey preprint
- **Peer-reviewed status:** No confirmed accepted venue
- **Thesis section:** S5.3 — Planning, Search, Memory, and Long-Horizon Decision-Making for LLM Agents
- **Main category:** efficient agents survey: memory, tool learning, planning
- **S5.3 role:** memory and long-horizon context management
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Support/recent-trend citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Low to medium
- **BibTeX key:** `efficientmemorytoollearning2026`

---

## Simple understanding

Toward Efficient Agents: Memory, Tool Learning, and Planning is a survey or position-style paper about planning, reasoning, memory, or agent efficiency. Its main value is taxonomy and synthesis rather than a new agent algorithm.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Web/GUI/computer-use agents fail when they act reactively, forget context, cannot recover, or explore inefficiently.
Paper → This work adds planning, search, memory, reflection, world modeling, skill reuse, or context management.
Goal → Make the agent more reliable on long-horizon interactive tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the paper changes inside the agent decision loop**.  
Only after that should it be connected to S5.3 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Organize the literature around planning/reasoning/memory/tool-learning methods for LLM-based agents.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
efficient agents survey: memory, tool learning, planning
```

This belongs to the agent planning layer:

```text
task instruction
→ current observation/state
→ planning or search
→ candidate actions
→ evaluation/reflection/correction
→ execution
→ memory/context update
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Agent planning is not a single technique: it includes decomposition, search, reflection, memory, tool use, context management, and evaluation.

For S5.3, the important question is:

```text
How does the method improve decision-making beyond one-step ReAct-style action selection?
```

Typical S5.3 improvements include:

- planning ahead,
- exploring multiple branches,
- reflecting on failure,
- backtracking after mistakes,
- using memory and replay,
- summarizing long contexts,
- learning reusable skills,
- simulating action consequences,
- and allocating interaction budget more intelligently.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **Taxonomy or framework figure.**
- **Comparison table of planning/reasoning/memory methods.**
- **Open challenges and future directions.**

Also extract, if available:

- planning/search algorithm,
- memory representation,
- action space,
- benchmark and task horizon,
- success-rate improvement,
- compute/cost trade-off,
- ablation study,
- and failure modes.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** Survey papers are secondary sources and should not replace primary method papers.
- **Limitation 2:** Fast-moving agent research can make survey taxonomies outdated quickly.
- **Limitation 3:** The paper may be broad and not focused only on web automation.

General thesis-level limitation:

```text
Better planning does not automatically mean generalized web automation is solved.
The agent still needs perception, grounding, execution, verification, recovery, and safety.
```

Therefore, this paper should be used as **P2 support**, not as the only foundation for S5.3.

---

This should be cited as a preprint, technical report, position paper, or submission unless a later peer-reviewed version is confirmed.

# S5.3 P2 Paper 19 — Web World Models

## Metadata

- **Title:** Web World Models
- **Year:** 2025
- **Verified venue/status:** arXiv 2512.23676
- **Peer-reviewed status:** No confirmed accepted venue
- **Thesis section:** S5.3 — Planning, Search, Memory, and Long-Horizon Decision-Making for LLM Agents
- **Main category:** web world models / deterministic web physics + LLM imagination
- **S5.3 role:** world-model-based prediction and action correction
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Support/recent-trend citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Low to medium
- **BibTeX key:** `webworld2025`

---

## Simple understanding

Web World Models proposes a middle-ground environment design between fixed database-backed web apps and fully generative world models. The core idea is to let deterministic web code define the world's state and physics, while LLMs generate narratives, descriptions, and high-level content on top.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Web/GUI/computer-use agents fail when they act reactively, forget context, cannot recover, or explore inefficiently.
Paper → This work adds planning, search, memory, reflection, world modeling, skill reuse, or context management.
Goal → Make the agent more reliable on long-horizon interactive tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the paper changes inside the agent decision loop**.  
Only after that should it be connected to S5.3 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Use ordinary web stacks as controllable world-model substrates: code defines rules, typed interfaces define latent state, and LLMs add bounded imagination.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
web world models / deterministic web physics + LLM imagination
```

This belongs to the agent planning layer:

```text
task instruction
→ current observation/state
→ planning or search
→ candidate actions
→ evaluation/reflection/correction
→ execution
→ memory/context update
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

The paper argues that web stacks can host scalable, open-ended, yet controllable environments by separating deterministic physics from stochastic LLM imagination.

For S5.3, the important question is:

```text
How does the method improve decision-making beyond one-step ReAct-style action selection?
```

Typical S5.3 improvements include:

- planning ahead,
- exploring multiple branches,
- reflecting on failure,
- backtracking after mistakes,
- using memory and replay,
- summarizing long contexts,
- learning reusable skills,
- simulating action consequences,
- and allocating interaction budget more intelligently.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **Figure 1 contrasts fixed web frameworks, Web World Models, and fully generative world models.**
- **The four design principles: separation of concerns, typed interfaces, deterministic generation, and graceful degradation.**
- **Examples including Infinite Travel Atlas, Galaxy Travel Atlas, AI Spire, and AI Alchemy.**

Also extract, if available:

- planning/search algorithm,
- memory representation,
- action space,
- benchmark and task horizon,
- success-rate improvement,
- compute/cost trade-off,
- ablation study,
- and failure modes.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** It is an arXiv preprint with no confirmed accepted venue.
- **Limitation 2:** It is more conceptual and demonstrative than a benchmarked web-agent planning method.
- **Limitation 3:** It builds environments for agents, but does not directly solve real-world browser automation or data extraction.

General thesis-level limitation:

```text
Better planning does not automatically mean generalized web automation is solved.
The agent still needs perception, grounding, execution, verification, recovery, and safety.
```

Therefore, this paper should be used as **P2 support**, not as the only foundation for S5.3.

---

# S5.3 P2 Paper 20 — Agent Alpha: Tree Search Unifying Generation, Exploration and Evaluation for Computer-Use Agents

## Metadata

- **Title:** Agent Alpha: Tree Search Unifying Generation, Exploration and Evaluation for Computer-Use Agents
- **Year:** 2026
- **Verified venue/status:** arXiv 2602.02995
- **Peer-reviewed status:** No confirmed accepted venue
- **Thesis section:** S5.3 — Planning, Search, Memory, and Long-Horizon Decision-Making for LLM Agents
- **Main category:** step-level MCTS / alpha-UCT for computer-use agents
- **S5.3 role:** search-based and exploration-based planning
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Support/recent-trend citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Low to medium
- **BibTeX key:** `alphatreesearchunifying2026`

---

## Simple understanding

Agent Alpha is a tree-search framework for computer-use agents. Instead of running one linear trajectory or many independent trajectories, it uses step-level Monte Carlo Tree Search so the agent can reuse good prefixes, prune bad branches, and recover from early mistakes.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Web/GUI/computer-use agents fail when they act reactively, forget context, cannot recover, or explore inefficiently.
Paper → This work adds planning, search, memory, reflection, world modeling, skill reuse, or context management.
Goal → Make the agent more reliable on long-horizon interactive tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the paper changes inside the agent decision loop**.  
Only after that should it be connected to S5.3 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Unify generation, exploration, and evaluation through step-level MCTS guided by Alpha-UCT, comparison-driven evaluation, and diversity-constrained expansion.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
step-level MCTS / alpha-UCT for computer-use agents
```

This belongs to the agent planning layer:

```text
task instruction
→ current observation/state
→ planning or search
→ candidate actions
→ evaluation/reflection/correction
→ execution
→ memory/context update
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

The paper reports about 77% success on OSWorld, outperforming trajectory-level baselines under comparable compute, but with higher runtime cost.

For S5.3, the important question is:

```text
How does the method improve decision-making beyond one-step ReAct-style action selection?
```

Typical S5.3 improvements include:

- planning ahead,
- exploring multiple branches,
- reflecting on failure,
- backtracking after mistakes,
- using memory and replay,
- summarizing long contexts,
- learning reusable skills,
- simulating action consequences,
- and allocating interaction budget more intelligently.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **Figure 1 contrasts wide scaling, tree search, and Agent Alpha's search framework.**
- **Alpha-UCT and max-Q back-propagation for dependent evaluations.**
- **Table 1 reports 77.29% average success on OSWorld.**

Also extract, if available:

- planning/search algorithm,
- memory representation,
- action space,
- benchmark and task horizon,
- success-rate improvement,
- compute/cost trade-off,
- ablation study,
- and failure modes.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** It is an arXiv preprint with no confirmed accepted venue.
- **Limitation 2:** MCTS improves planning but increases inference cost and latency.
- **Limitation 3:** OSWorld computer-use results may not directly transfer to live web environments.

General thesis-level limitation:

```text
Better planning does not automatically mean generalized web automation is solved.
The agent still needs perception, grounding, execution, verification, recovery, and safety.
```

Therefore, this paper should be used as **P2 support**, not as the only foundation for S5.3.

---

## Venue/status caution

This should be cited as a preprint, technical report, position paper, or submission unless a later peer-reviewed version is confirmed.

For final thesis writing:


# S5.3 P2 Paper 21 — World-Model-Augmented Web Agents with Action Correction / WAC

## Metadata

- **Title:** World-Model-Augmented Web Agents with Action Correction / WAC
- **Year:** 2026
- **Verified venue/status:** arXiv 2602.15384
- **Peer-reviewed status:** No confirmed accepted venue
- **Thesis section:** S5.3 — Planning, Search, Memory, and Long-Horizon Decision-Making for LLM Agents
- **Main category:** world-model action correction for web agents
- **S5.3 role:** world-model-based prediction and action correction
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Support/recent-trend citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Low to medium
- **BibTeX key:** `worldmodelaugmentedweb2026`

---

## Simple understanding

WAC adds a world model to a web agent before execution. The world model helps propose better actions, simulates possible outcomes, and triggers correction when candidate actions look risky or inadequate.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Web/GUI/computer-use agents fail when they act reactively, forget context, cannot recover, or explore inefficiently.
Paper → This work adds planning, search, memory, reflection, world modeling, skill reuse, or context management.
Goal → Make the agent more reliable on long-horizon interactive tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the paper changes inside the agent decision loop**.  
Only after that should it be connected to S5.3 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Combine collaborative action generation with world-model-centered action deduction and feedback-driven action refinement.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
world-model action correction for web agents
```

This belongs to the agent planning layer:

```text
task instruction
→ current observation/state
→ planning or search
→ candidate actions
→ evaluation/reflection/correction
→ execution
→ memory/context update
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

WAC improves VisualWebArena and Online-Mind2Web performance by using world-model guidance, simulated outcomes, and judge-based feedback before executing actions.

For S5.3, the important question is:

```text
How does the method improve decision-making beyond one-step ReAct-style action selection?
```

Typical S5.3 improvements include:

- planning ahead,
- exploring multiple branches,
- reflecting on failure,
- backtracking after mistakes,
- using memory and replay,
- summarizing long contexts,
- learning reusable skills,
- simulating action consequences,
- and allocating interaction budget more intelligently.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **Figure 2 shows Stage I Action Generation and Stage II Action Deduction.**
- **Algorithm 1 details router, world-model guidance, candidate action filtering, simulation, judging, refinement, and execution.**
- **Table 1 reports results on VisualWebArena and Online-Mind2Web.**

Also extract, if available:

- planning/search algorithm,
- memory representation,
- action space,
- benchmark and task horizon,
- success-rate improvement,
- compute/cost trade-off,
- ablation study,
- and failure modes.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** It is an arXiv preprint with no confirmed accepted venue.
- **Limitation 2:** Reported gains are modest: 1.8% on VisualWebArena and 1.3% on Online-Mind2Web.
- **Limitation 3:** The approach depends on the quality of the world model and judge model.

General thesis-level limitation:

```text
Better planning does not automatically mean generalized web automation is solved.
The agent still needs perception, grounding, execution, verification, recovery, and safety.
```

Therefore, this paper should be used as **P2 support**, not as the only foundation for S5.3.

---

This should be cited as a preprint, technical report, position paper, or submission unless a later peer-reviewed version is confirmed.

```text
system/demo paper → useful architecture/tooling citation
```

# S5.3 P2 Paper 22 — AI Planning Framework for LLM-Based Web Agents

## Metadata

- **Title:** AI Planning Framework for LLM-Based Web Agents
- **Year:** 2026
- **Verified venue/status:** arXiv 2603.12710 / Manuscript submitted to ACM
- **Peer-reviewed status:** No confirmed accepted venue
- **Thesis section:** S5.3 — Planning, Search, Memory, and Long-Horizon Decision-Making for LLM Agents
- **Main category:** planning taxonomy and trajectory metrics for web agents
- **S5.3 role:** planning taxonomy and synthesis
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Support/recent-trend citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Low to medium
- **BibTeX key:** `aiplanningframeworkllm2026`

---

## Simple understanding

This paper formalizes LLM-based web agents as planning systems and maps modern agent architectures to classical planning strategies. It also proposes trajectory-level metrics beyond binary success/failure.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Web/GUI/computer-use agents fail when they act reactively, forget context, cannot recover, or explore inefficiently.
Paper → This work adds planning, search, memory, reflection, world modeling, skill reuse, or context management.
Goal → Make the agent more reliable on long-horizon interactive tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the paper changes inside the agent decision loop**.  
Only after that should it be connected to S5.3 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Connect step-by-step agents, tree-search agents, and full-plan-in-advance agents to planning paradigms, then evaluate trajectories with process-level metrics.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
planning taxonomy and trajectory metrics for web agents
```

This belongs to the agent planning layer:

```text
task instruction
→ current observation/state
→ planning or search
→ candidate actions
→ evaluation/reflection/correction
→ execution
→ memory/context update
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

The paper shows that different planning paradigms have different strengths: step-by-step agents align better with human trajectories, while full-plan-in-advance agents can achieve high element accuracy.

For S5.3, the important question is:

```text
How does the method improve decision-making beyond one-step ReAct-style action selection?
```

Typical S5.3 improvements include:

- planning ahead,
- exploring multiple branches,
- reflecting on failure,
- backtracking after mistakes,
- using memory and replay,
- summarizing long contexts,
- learning reusable skills,
- simulating action consequences,
- and allocating interaction budget more intelligently.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **Taxonomy mapping Step-by-Step to breadth-oriented traversal, Tree Search to best-first search, and Full-Plan-in-Advance to depth-oriented traversal.**
- **794 human-labeled WebArena trajectories.**
- **Five metrics: Recovery Rate, Repetitiveness Rate, Step Success Rate, Partial Success Rate, and Element Accuracy Rate.**

Also extract, if available:

- planning/search algorithm,
- memory representation,
- action space,
- benchmark and task horizon,
- success-rate improvement,
- compute/cost trade-off,
- ablation study,
- and failure modes.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** It is an arXiv manuscript submitted to ACM, not confirmed accepted.
- **Limitation 2:** It focuses on planning diagnosis and metrics rather than a new high-performing web agent.
- **Limitation 3:** LLM-as-judge metrics require validation and can introduce evaluator bias.

General thesis-level limitation:

```text
Better planning does not automatically mean generalized web automation is solved.
The agent still needs perception, grounding, execution, verification, recovery, and safety.
```

Therefore, this paper should be used as **P2 support**, not as the only foundation for S5.3.

# S5.3 P2 Paper 23 — MANGO: Multi-Agent Web Navigation via Global-View Optimization

## Metadata

- **Title:** MANGO: Multi-Agent Web Navigation via Global-View Optimization
- **Year:** 2026
- **Verified venue/status:** arXiv 2604.18779
- **Peer-reviewed status:** No confirmed accepted venue
- **Thesis section:** S5.3 — Planning, Search, Memory, and Long-Horizon Decision-Making for LLM Agents
- **Main category:** global-view optimization / Thompson sampling for web navigation
- **S5.3 role:** global-view navigation and budget allocation
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Support/recent-trend citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Low to medium
- **BibTeX key:** `mangomultiwebnavigation2026`

---

## Simple understanding

MANGO improves web navigation by giving agents a global view of the website before navigation. Instead of always starting from the homepage, it crawls/searches for candidate URLs and uses Thompson Sampling to allocate navigation attempts.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Web/GUI/computer-use agents fail when they act reactively, forget context, cannot recover, or explore inefficiently.
Paper → This work adds planning, search, memory, reflection, world modeling, skill reuse, or context management.
Goal → Make the agent more reliable on long-horizon interactive tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the paper changes inside the agent decision loop**.  
Only after that should it be connected to S5.3 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Use global website structure, URL candidate generation, multi-armed bandit URL selection, and episodic memory to improve web navigation efficiency.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
global-view optimization / Thompson sampling for web navigation
```

This belongs to the agent planning layer:

```text
task instruction
→ current observation/state
→ planning or search
→ candidate actions
→ evaluation/reflection/correction
→ execution
→ memory/context update
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

MANGO reports higher success on WebVoyager and WebWalkerQA, especially for complex websites where starting from the root URL wastes budget.

For S5.3, the important question is:

```text
How does the method improve decision-making beyond one-step ReAct-style action selection?
```

Typical S5.3 improvements include:

- planning ahead,

- summarizing long contexts,

- and allocating interaction budget more intelligently.

- **Figure 1 shows global structure analysis, MAB URL selection, web navigation, reflection, and episodic memory.**
- **Thompson Sampling over candidate URLs with Beta distributions.**
- **Reported 63.6% success on WebVoyager with GPT-5-mini and 52.5% on WebWalkerQA.**

Also extract, if available:

- planning/search algorithm,
- memory representation,
- action space,
- benchmark and task horizon,
- success-rate improvement,
- compute/cost trade-off,
- ablation study,
- and failure modes.

---

- **Limitation 1:** It is an arXiv preprint with no confirmed accepted venue.
- **Limitation 2:** It depends on crawling/search access and may be limited by dynamic or private websites.

## Simple understanding

WebXSkill teaches autonomous web agents reusable executable skills. Each skill pairs a parameterized action program with step-level natural-language guidance, bridging the gap between textual workflows and opaque code skills.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Web/GUI/computer-use agents fail when they act reactively, forget context, cannot recover, or explore inefficiently.
```

# S5.3 P2 Paper 25 — LiteWebAgent: The Open-Source Suite for VLM-Based Web-Agent Applications

## Metadata

- **Title:** LiteWebAgent: The Open-Source Suite for VLM-Based Web-Agent Applications
- **Year:** 2025
- **Verified venue/status:** NAACL 2025 System Demonstrations
- **Peer-reviewed status:** Yes, demo
- **Thesis section:** S5.3 — Planning, Search, Memory, and Long-Horizon Decision-Making for LLM Agents
- **Main category:** open-source web-agent suite with planning/memory/tree search
- **S5.3 role:** search-based and exploration-based planning
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Medium system/demo citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Medium to high
- **BibTeX key:** `litewebagentopensourcesuite2025`

---

## Simple understanding

LiteWebAgent: The Open-Source Suite for VLM-Based Web-Agent Applications studies search or exploration for agents. It is important because web and GUI agents often fail when they commit too early to one path without exploring alternatives.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Web/GUI/computer-use agents fail when they act reactively, forget context, cannot recover, or explore inefficiently.
Paper → This work adds planning, search, memory, reflection, world modeling, skill reuse, or context management.
Goal → Make the agent more reliable on long-horizon interactive tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the paper changes inside the agent decision loop**.  
Only after that should it be connected to S5.3 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Improve agent planning by exploring multiple possible action trajectories and selecting more promising paths using search, reflection, or value estimates.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
open-source web-agent suite with planning/memory/tree search
```

This belongs to the agent planning layer:

```text
task instruction
→ current observation/state
→ planning or search
→ candidate actions
→ evaluation/reflection/correction
→ execution
→ memory/context update
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Search-based or exploration-based planning can improve long-horizon task success, but it usually increases compute cost and requires reliable evaluation signals.

For S5.3, the important question is:

```text
How does the method improve decision-making beyond one-step ReAct-style action selection?
```

Typical S5.3 improvements include:

- planning ahead,
- exploring multiple branches,
- reflecting on failure,
- backtracking after mistakes,
- using memory and replay,
- summarizing long contexts,
- learning reusable skills,
- simulating action consequences,
- and allocating interaction budget more intelligently.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **Search/exploration algorithm design.**
- **Benchmark success-rate improvement over reactive baselines.**
- **Ablations showing the effect of search, reflection, or evaluation components.**

Also extract, if available:

- planning/search algorithm,
- memory representation,
- action space,
- benchmark and task horizon,
- success-rate improvement,
- compute/cost trade-off,
- ablation study,
- and failure modes.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** Search increases latency and token/tool cost.
- **Limitation 2:** Value estimation or reflection can be noisy.
- **Limitation 3:** Search over web/GUI environments can become expensive because actions change external state.

General thesis-level limitation:

```text
Better planning does not automatically mean generalized web automation is solved.
The agent still needs perception, grounding, execution, verification, recovery, and safety.
```

Therefore, this paper should be used as **P2 support**, not as the only foundation for S5.3.

---

This is a peer-reviewed system/demo source. It is useful for tooling and architecture evidence, but weaker than a main conference research paper.

For final thesis writing:

```text

system/demo paper → useful architecture/tooling citation
arXiv/technical report/submission → recent trend; cite cautiously
```

## Source: `S5_3_P2\paper_first_notes\S5_3_P2_GLOBAL_SYNTHESIS.md`

# S5.3 P2 — Global Synthesis

## What this S5.3 P2 batch covers

The S5.3 P2 papers focus on the **planning and decision-making layer** of LLM-based agents.

They cover:

1. Strategic exploration for web tasks.
2. Reflective MCTS and tree search.
3. Premeditated or plan-ahead actions.
4. Reflective memory and replay.
5. LLM planning surveys and taxonomies.
6. Episodic memory for long-term agents.
7. Process rewards at inference time.
8. Programmatic skills and executable workflows.
9. Deliberative GUI reasoning.
10. Error detection, backtracking, and correction.
11. Goal-state reflection.
12. Test-time interaction scaling.
13. Cognitive reasoning for web agents.
14. Context summarization and program-guided context.
15. Web world models and world-model-based action correction.
16. Global-view web navigation with bandit allocation.
17. Skill learning for autonomous web agents.

## Main thesis value

This batch is important because generalized web automation is fundamentally long-horizon.

A web/data-extraction agent must often:

```text
understand the task
→ find the right site/page
→ choose a promising navigation path
→ interact with forms and UI elements
→ recover from wrong clicks
→ remember previous attempts
→ reuse common workflows
→ verify extracted information
```

S5.3 should therefore explain why one-step reactive agents are insufficient.

## Recommended backbone sources

Use peer-reviewed sources as stronger support:

```text
WebPilot — AAAI 2025
ExACT — ICLR 2025
R2D2 — ACL 2025
PlanGenLLMs — ACL 2025
BacktrackAgent — EMNLP 2025
ReflAct — EMNLP 2025
LiteWebAgent — NAACL 2025 System Demonstrations
```

## Use as recent trends / technical directions

Use the following carefully as preprints or technical reports:

```text
PAFFA
Episodic Memory position paper
Process Rewards for GUI Navigation
ASI
InfiGUI-R1
SteP
Thinking vs. Doing
Web-CogReasoner
ReSum
AgentProg
Toward Efficient Agents
Agentic Reasoning
Web World Models
Agent Alpha
WAC
AI Planning Framework
MANGO
WebXSkill
```

## Key synthesis argument

A strong S5.3 paragraph can say:

```text
Planning in LLM-based agents has evolved from reactive ReAct-style loops toward search, reflection, memory, and reusable procedural abstractions. 
Recent methods explore MCTS and tree search, reflective backtracking, process rewards, world-model simulation, context summarization, global-view navigation, and executable skill reuse. 
This evolution reflects a central limitation of web agents: long-horizon web automation requires not only language understanding, but also explicit mechanisms for exploration, error recovery, memory, and action reuse.
```

## Suggested subsection organization

1. Reactive planning and its limitations.
2. Search-based planning and tree exploration.
3. Reflection, backtracking, and action correction.
4. Memory and context management for long-horizon interaction.
5. World models and consequence simulation.
6. Skill learning and programmatic action abstraction.
7. Planning evaluation and taxonomy.

## Final recommendation

Keep all 25 papers in S5.3 P2. In final thesis writing, use the peer-reviewed papers for core claims and the 2025–2026 preprints for emerging trends and research gaps.

## Interpretation for S5.3

S5.3 P2 is highly relevant to the planning layer of LLM-based web and GUI agents. The batch shows a clear evolution:

```text
hierarchical web-action policies
→ MCTS/tree-search web agents
→ reflective search and backtracking
→ memory/replay and episodic planning
→ process rewards and RL for long-horizon interaction
→ context summarization and program-guided context management
→ world-model/action-correction and executable skills
```

For thesis writing, use peer-reviewed sources such as WebPilot, ExACT, R2D2, PlanGenLLMs, BacktrackAgent, ReflAct, and LiteWebAgent as stronger citation support. Use the 2025–2026 arXiv papers mainly for emerging directions, especially world models, skill learning, bandit-based global navigation, and long-horizon context management.

## Source: `S5_3_P2_paper_first_detailed_markdown_notes\S5_3_P2_01_WebPilot_A_Versatile_and_Autonomous_Multi-Agent_System_for_Web_Task_Execution_with_Strategic_Exploration.md`

# S5.3 P2 Paper 01 — WebPilot: A Versatile and Autonomous Multi-Agent System for Web Task Execution with Strategic Exploration

## Metadata

- **Title:** WebPilot: A Versatile and Autonomous Multi-Agent System for Web Task Execution with Strategic Exploration
- **Year:** 2024/2025
- **Verified venue/status:** AAAI 2025
- **Peer-reviewed status:** Yes
- **Thesis section:** S5.3 — Planning, Search, Memory, and Long-Horizon Decision-Making for LLM Agents
- **Main category:** strategic exploration / multi-agent web task execution
- **S5.3 role:** search-based and exploration-based planning
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Strong citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Medium to high
- **BibTeX key:** `webpilotversatileautonomousmulti2024`

---

## Simple understanding

WebPilot: A Versatile and Autonomous Multi-Agent System for Web Task Execution with Strategic Exploration studies search or exploration for agents. It is important because web and GUI agents often fail when they commit too early to one path without exploring alternatives.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Web/GUI/computer-use agents fail when they act reactively, forget context, cannot recover, or explore inefficiently.
Paper → This work adds planning, search, memory, reflection, world modeling, skill reuse, or context management.
Goal → Make the agent more reliable on long-horizon interactive tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the paper changes inside the agent decision loop**.  
Only after that should it be connected to S5.3 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Improve agent planning by exploring multiple possible action trajectories and selecting more promising paths using search, reflection, or value estimates.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
strategic exploration / multi-agent web task execution
```

This belongs to the agent planning layer:

```text
task instruction
→ current observation/state
→ planning or search
→ candidate actions
→ evaluation/reflection/correction
→ execution
→ memory/context update
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Search-based or exploration-based planning can improve long-horizon task success, but it usually increases compute cost and requires reliable evaluation signals.

For S5.3, the important question is:

```text
How does the method improve decision-making beyond one-step ReAct-style action selection?
```

Typical S5.3 improvements include:

- planning ahead,
- exploring multiple branches,
- reflecting on failure,
- backtracking after mistakes,
- using memory and replay,
- summarizing long contexts,
- learning reusable skills,
- simulating action consequences,
- and allocating interaction budget more intelligently.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **Search/exploration algorithm design.**
- **Benchmark success-rate improvement over reactive baselines.**
- **Ablations showing the effect of search, reflection, or evaluation components.**

Also extract, if available:

- planning/search algorithm,
- memory representation,
- action space,
- benchmark and task horizon,
- success-rate improvement,
- compute/cost trade-off,
- ablation study,
- and failure modes.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** Search increases latency and token/tool cost.
- **Limitation 2:** Value estimation or reflection can be noisy.
- **Limitation 3:** Search over web/GUI environments can become expensive because actions change external state.

General thesis-level limitation:

```text
Better planning does not automatically mean generalized web automation is solved.
The agent still needs perception, grounding, execution, verification, recovery, and safety.
```

Therefore, this paper should be used as **P2 support**, not as the only foundation for S5.3.

---

## Venue/status caution

This is safe for stronger thesis claims because the verified venue/status is **AAAI 2025**.

For final thesis writing:

```text
peer-reviewed main conference/journal → stronger citation
system/demo paper → useful architecture/tooling citation
arXiv/technical report/submission → recent trend; cite cautiously
```

---

## Relation to S5.3

This paper belongs in **S5.3** because S5.3 discusses the planning and decision-making layer of LLM-based agents.

Its role is:

```text
WebPilot: A Versatile and Autonomous Multi-Agent System for Web Task Execution with Strategic Exploration
→ search-based and exploration-based planning
→ P2 support for planning/search/memory/long-horizon decision-making
```

Use it after explaining its mechanism.  
Do not introduce it only as “P2”; introduce the planning problem first.

---

## Relation to the thesis topic

Your thesis topic is:


# S5.3 P2 Paper 02 — ExACT: Teaching AI Agents to Explore with Reflective-MCTS and Exploratory Learning

## Metadata

- **Title:** ExACT: Teaching AI Agents to Explore with Reflective-MCTS and Exploratory Learning
- **Year:** 2024/2025
- **Verified venue/status:** ICLR 2025
- **Peer-reviewed status:** Yes
- **Thesis section:** S5.3 — Planning, Search, Memory, and Long-Horizon Decision-Making for LLM Agents
- **Main category:** Reflective-MCTS / exploratory learning
- **S5.3 role:** search-based and exploration-based planning
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Strong citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Medium to high
- **BibTeX key:** `exactteachingaiexplore2024`

---

## Simple understanding

ExACT: Teaching AI Agents to Explore with Reflective-MCTS and Exploratory Learning studies search or exploration for agents. It is important because web and GUI agents often fail when they commit too early to one path without exploring alternatives.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Web/GUI/computer-use agents fail when they act reactively, forget context, cannot recover, or explore inefficiently.
Paper → This work adds planning, search, memory, reflection, world modeling, skill reuse, or context management.
Goal → Make the agent more reliable on long-horizon interactive tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the paper changes inside the agent decision loop**.  
Only after that should it be connected to S5.3 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Improve agent planning by exploring multiple possible action trajectories and selecting more promising paths using search, reflection, or value estimates.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
Reflective-MCTS / exploratory learning
```

This belongs to the agent planning layer:

```text
task instruction
→ current observation/state
→ planning or search
→ candidate actions
→ evaluation/reflection/correction
→ execution
→ memory/context update
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Search-based or exploration-based planning can improve long-horizon task success, but it usually increases compute cost and requires reliable evaluation signals.

For S5.3, the important question is:

```text
How does the method improve decision-making beyond one-step ReAct-style action selection?
```

Typical S5.3 improvements include:

- planning ahead,
- exploring multiple branches,
- reflecting on failure,
- backtracking after mistakes,
- using memory and replay,
- summarizing long contexts,
- learning reusable skills,
- simulating action consequences,
- and allocating interaction budget more intelligently.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **Search/exploration algorithm design.**
- **Benchmark success-rate improvement over reactive baselines.**
- **Ablations showing the effect of search, reflection, or evaluation components.**

Also extract, if available:

- planning/search algorithm,
- memory representation,
- action space,
- benchmark and task horizon,
- success-rate improvement,
- compute/cost trade-off,
- ablation study,
- and failure modes.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** Search increases latency and token/tool cost.
- **Limitation 2:** Value estimation or reflection can be noisy.
- **Limitation 3:** Search over web/GUI environments can become expensive because actions change external state.

General thesis-level limitation:

```text
Better planning does not automatically mean generalized web automation is solved.
The agent still needs perception, grounding, execution, verification, recovery, and safety.
```

Therefore, this paper should be used as **P2 support**, not as the only foundation for S5.3.

---

This is safe for stronger thesis claims because the verified venue/status is **ICLR 2025**.

# S5.3 P2 Paper 06 — Position: Episodic Memory is the Missing Piece for Long-Term LLM Agents

## Metadata

- **Title:** Position: Episodic Memory is the Missing Piece for Long-Term LLM Agents
- **Year:** 2025
- **Verified venue/status:** arXiv 2502.06975
- **Peer-reviewed status:** No confirmed accepted venue
- **Thesis section:** S5.3 — Planning, Search, Memory, and Long-Horizon Decision-Making for LLM Agents
- **Main category:** episodic memory position paper
- **S5.3 role:** memory and long-horizon context management
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Support/recent-trend citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Low to medium
- **BibTeX key:** `positionepisodicmemoryis2025`

---

## Simple understanding

Position: Episodic Memory is the Missing Piece for Long-Term LLM Agents focuses on memory or context management for agents. It matters because long-horizon agents must remember goals, past attempts, errors, and useful procedures without overloading the context window.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Web/GUI/computer-use agents fail when they act reactively, forget context, cannot recover, or explore inefficiently.
Paper → This work adds planning, search, memory, reflection, world modeling, skill reuse, or context management.
Goal → Make the agent more reliable on long-horizon interactive tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the paper changes inside the agent decision loop**.  
Only after that should it be connected to S5.3 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Use memory, replay, summarization, or program-guided context to preserve relevant information during long-horizon interaction.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
episodic memory position paper
```

This belongs to the agent planning layer:

```text
task instruction
→ current observation/state
→ planning or search
→ candidate actions
→ evaluation/reflection/correction
→ execution
→ memory/context update
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Memory/context mechanisms can reduce repeated mistakes and help agents maintain task progress, but they can also introduce stale or compressed information errors.

For S5.3, the important question is:

```text
How does the method improve decision-making beyond one-step ReAct-style action selection?
```

Typical S5.3 improvements include:

- planning ahead,
- exploring multiple branches,
- reflecting on failure,
- backtracking after mistakes,
- using memory and replay,
- summarizing long contexts,
- learning reusable skills,
- simulating action consequences,
- and allocating interaction budget more intelligently.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **Memory architecture or context-management pipeline.**
- **Long-horizon benchmark results.**
- **Ablation showing contribution of memory/summarization/replay.**

Also extract, if available:

- planning/search algorithm,
- memory representation,
- action space,
- benchmark and task horizon,
- success-rate improvement,
- compute/cost trade-off,
- ablation study,
- and failure modes.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** Memory quality depends on what is stored, summarized, and retrieved.
- **Limitation 2:** Bad or stale memories can mislead the agent.
- **Limitation 3:** Memory mechanisms add complexity and can increase cost.

General thesis-level limitation:

```text
Better planning does not automatically mean generalized web automation is solved.
The agent still needs perception, grounding, execution, verification, recovery, and safety.
```

Therefore, this paper should be used as **P2 support**, not as the only foundation for S5.3.

---

## Simple understanding

Inducing Programmatic Skills for Agentic Tasks / ASI studies programmatic or reusable skills for agents. It matters because long web tasks often contain repeated procedures that should be abstracted instead of replanned every time.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Web/GUI/computer-use agents fail when they act reactively, forget context, cannot recover, or explore inefficiently.
Paper → This work adds planning, search, memory, reflection, world modeling, skill reuse, or context management.

```

The first goal is to understand **what the paper changes inside the agent decision loop**.

```text
→ memory/context update
```

```text

```

# S5.3 P2 Paper 10 — BacktrackAgent: Enhancing GUI Agent with Error Detection and Backtracking Mechanism

## Metadata

- **Title:** BacktrackAgent: Enhancing GUI Agent with Error Detection and Backtracking Mechanism
- **Year:** 2025
- **Verified venue/status:** EMNLP 2025 Main Conference
- **Peer-reviewed status:** Yes
- **Thesis section:** S5.3 — Planning, Search, Memory, and Long-Horizon Decision-Making for LLM Agents
- **Main category:** error detection and backtracking for GUI agents
- **S5.3 role:** reflection, error detection, and recovery
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Strong citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Medium to high
- **BibTeX key:** `backtrackagentenhancingguierror2025`

---

## Simple understanding

BacktrackAgent: Enhancing GUI Agent with Error Detection and Backtracking Mechanism studies reflection, world modeling, action correction, or backtracking. It matters because agents need to detect mistakes and reason about consequences before or after acting.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Web/GUI/computer-use agents fail when they act reactively, forget context, cannot recover, or explore inefficiently.
Paper → This work adds planning, search, memory, reflection, world modeling, skill reuse, or context management.
Goal → Make the agent more reliable on long-horizon interactive tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the paper changes inside the agent decision loop**.  
Only after that should it be connected to S5.3 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Add reflective or predictive mechanisms that evaluate states/actions and correct poor decisions.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
error detection and backtracking for GUI agents
```

This belongs to the agent planning layer:

```text
task instruction
→ current observation/state
→ planning or search
→ candidate actions
→ evaluation/reflection/correction
→ execution
→ memory/context update
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

Reflection and world-modeling can improve robustness, but their quality depends on accurate state prediction and reliable judging.

For S5.3, the important question is:

```text
How does the method improve decision-making beyond one-step ReAct-style action selection?
```

Typical S5.3 improvements include:

- planning ahead,
- exploring multiple branches,
- reflecting on failure,
- backtracking after mistakes,
- using memory and replay,
- summarizing long contexts,
- learning reusable skills,
- simulating action consequences,
- and allocating interaction budget more intelligently.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **Reflection/correction architecture.**
- **Before/after ablation results.**
- **Failure cases showing improved recovery.**

Also extract, if available:

- planning/search algorithm,
- memory representation,
- action space,
- benchmark and task horizon,
- success-rate improvement,
- compute/cost trade-off,
- ablation study,
- and failure modes.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** World models and reflections can hallucinate.
- **Limitation 2:** Correction loops increase latency.
- **Limitation 3:** Backtracking may be unavailable or unsafe in real websites.

General thesis-level limitation:

```text
Better planning does not automatically mean generalized web automation is solved.
The agent still needs perception, grounding, execution, verification, recovery, and safety.
```

Therefore, this paper should be used as **P2 support**, not as the only foundation for S5.3.

```text
Problem → Web/GUI/computer-use agents fail when they act reactively, forget context, cannot recover, or explore inefficiently.
Paper → This work adds planning, search, memory, reflection, world modeling, skill reuse, or context management.
```

# S5.3 P2 Paper 19 — Web World Models

## Metadata

- **Title:** Web World Models
- **Year:** 2025
- **Verified venue/status:** arXiv 2512.23676
- **Peer-reviewed status:** No confirmed accepted venue
- **Thesis section:** S5.3 — Planning, Search, Memory, and Long-Horizon Decision-Making for LLM Agents
- **Main category:** web world models / deterministic web physics + LLM imagination
- **S5.3 role:** world-model-based prediction and action correction
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Support/recent-trend citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Low to medium
- **BibTeX key:** `webworld2025`

---

## Simple understanding

Web World Models proposes a middle-ground environment design between fixed database-backed web apps and fully generative world models. The core idea is to let deterministic web code define the world's state and physics, while LLMs generate narratives, descriptions, and high-level content on top.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Web/GUI/computer-use agents fail when they act reactively, forget context, cannot recover, or explore inefficiently.
Paper → This work adds planning, search, memory, reflection, world modeling, skill reuse, or context management.
Goal → Make the agent more reliable on long-horizon interactive tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the paper changes inside the agent decision loop**.  
Only after that should it be connected to S5.3 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Use ordinary web stacks as controllable world-model substrates: code defines rules, typed interfaces define latent state, and LLMs add bounded imagination.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
web world models / deterministic web physics + LLM imagination
```

This belongs to the agent planning layer:

```text
task instruction
→ current observation/state
→ planning or search
→ candidate actions
→ evaluation/reflection/correction
→ execution
→ memory/context update
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

The paper argues that web stacks can host scalable, open-ended, yet controllable environments by separating deterministic physics from stochastic LLM imagination.

For S5.3, the important question is:

Typical S5.3 improvements include:

Also extract, if available:

- planning/search algorithm,
- memory representation,
- action space,
- benchmark and task horizon,
- success-rate improvement,
- compute/cost trade-off,
- ablation study,
- and failure modes.

---

- **Limitation 2:** It is more conceptual and demonstrative than a benchmarked web-agent planning method.

# S5.3 P2 Paper 20 — Agent Alpha: Tree Search Unifying Generation, Exploration and Evaluation for Computer-Use Agents

## Metadata

- **Title:** Agent Alpha: Tree Search Unifying Generation, Exploration and Evaluation for Computer-Use Agents
- **Year:** 2026
- **Verified venue/status:** arXiv 2602.02995
- **Peer-reviewed status:** No confirmed accepted venue
- **Thesis section:** S5.3 — Planning, Search, Memory, and Long-Horizon Decision-Making for LLM Agents
- **Main category:** step-level MCTS / alpha-UCT for computer-use agents
- **S5.3 role:** search-based and exploration-based planning
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Support/recent-trend citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Low to medium
- **BibTeX key:** `alphatreesearchunifying2026`

---

## Simple understanding

Agent Alpha is a tree-search framework for computer-use agents. Instead of running one linear trajectory or many independent trajectories, it uses step-level Monte Carlo Tree Search so the agent can reuse good prefixes, prune bad branches, and recover from early mistakes.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Web/GUI/computer-use agents fail when they act reactively, forget context, cannot recover, or explore inefficiently.
Paper → This work adds planning, search, memory, reflection, world modeling, skill reuse, or context management.
Goal → Make the agent more reliable on long-horizon interactive tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the paper changes inside the agent decision loop**.  
Only after that should it be connected to S5.3 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Unify generation, exploration, and evaluation through step-level MCTS guided by Alpha-UCT, comparison-driven evaluation, and diversity-constrained expansion.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
step-level MCTS / alpha-UCT for computer-use agents
```

This belongs to the agent planning layer:

```text
task instruction
→ current observation/state
→ planning or search
→ candidate actions
→ evaluation/reflection/correction
→ execution
→ memory/context update
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

The paper reports about 77% success on OSWorld, outperforming trajectory-level baselines under comparable compute, but with higher runtime cost.

For S5.3, the important question is:

```text
How does the method improve decision-making beyond one-step ReAct-style action selection?
```

Typical S5.3 improvements include:

- planning ahead,
- exploring multiple branches,

- simulating action consequences,
- and allocating interaction budget more intelligently.

- compute/cost trade-off,
- ablation study,
- and failure modes.

---

- **Limitation 1:** It is an arXiv preprint with no confirmed accepted venue.

# S5.3 P2 Paper 21 — World-Model-Augmented Web Agents with Action Correction / WAC

## Metadata

- **Title:** World-Model-Augmented Web Agents with Action Correction / WAC
- **Year:** 2026
- **Verified venue/status:** arXiv 2602.15384
- **Peer-reviewed status:** No confirmed accepted venue
- **Thesis section:** S5.3 — Planning, Search, Memory, and Long-Horizon Decision-Making for LLM Agents
- **Main category:** world-model action correction for web agents
- **S5.3 role:** world-model-based prediction and action correction
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Support/recent-trend citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Low to medium
- **BibTeX key:** `worldmodelaugmentedweb2026`

---

## Simple understanding

WAC adds a world model to a web agent before execution. The world model helps propose better actions, simulates possible outcomes, and triggers correction when candidate actions look risky or inadequate.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Web/GUI/computer-use agents fail when they act reactively, forget context, cannot recover, or explore inefficiently.
Paper → This work adds planning, search, memory, reflection, world modeling, skill reuse, or context management.
Goal → Make the agent more reliable on long-horizon interactive tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the paper changes inside the agent decision loop**.  
Only after that should it be connected to S5.3 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Combine collaborative action generation with world-model-centered action deduction and feedback-driven action refinement.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
world-model action correction for web agents
```

This belongs to the agent planning layer:

```text
task instruction
→ current observation/state
→ planning or search
→ candidate actions
→ evaluation/reflection/correction
→ execution
→ memory/context update
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

WAC improves VisualWebArena and Online-Mind2Web performance by using world-model guidance, simulated outcomes, and judge-based feedback before executing actions.

For S5.3, the important question is:

```text
How does the method improve decision-making beyond one-step ReAct-style action selection?
```

Typical S5.3 improvements include:

- planning ahead,
- exploring multiple branches,
- reflecting on failure,
- backtracking after mistakes,
- using memory and replay,
- summarizing long contexts,
- learning reusable skills,
- simulating action consequences,
- and allocating interaction budget more intelligently.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **Figure 2 shows Stage I Action Generation and Stage II Action Deduction.**
- **Algorithm 1 details router, world-model guidance, candidate action filtering, simulation, judging, refinement, and execution.**
- **Table 1 reports results on VisualWebArena and Online-Mind2Web.**

- action space,

- compute/cost trade-off,
- ablation study,
- and failure modes.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** It is an arXiv preprint with no confirmed accepted venue.
- **Limitation 2:** Reported gains are modest: 1.8% on VisualWebArena and 1.3% on Online-Mind2Web.
- **Limitation 3:** The approach depends on the quality of the world model and judge model.

General thesis-level limitation:

```text
Better planning does not automatically mean generalized web automation is solved.
The agent still needs perception, grounding, execution, verification, recovery, and safety.
```

Therefore, this paper should be used as **P2 support**, not as the only foundation for S5.3.

# S5.3 P2 Paper 22 — AI Planning Framework for LLM-Based Web Agents

## Metadata

- **Title:** AI Planning Framework for LLM-Based Web Agents
- **Year:** 2026
- **Verified venue/status:** arXiv 2603.12710 / Manuscript submitted to ACM
- **Peer-reviewed status:** No confirmed accepted venue
- **Thesis section:** S5.3 — Planning, Search, Memory, and Long-Horizon Decision-Making for LLM Agents
- **Main category:** planning taxonomy and trajectory metrics for web agents
- **S5.3 role:** planning taxonomy and synthesis
- **Priority:** P2
- **Recommended citation strength:** Support/recent-trend citation
- **Recommended reading depth:** Low to medium
- **BibTeX key:** `aiplanningframeworkllm2026`

---

## Simple understanding

This paper formalizes LLM-based web agents as planning systems and maps modern agent architectures to classical planning strategies. It also proposes trajectory-level metrics beyond binary success/failure.

In simple terms:

```text
Problem → Web/GUI/computer-use agents fail when they act reactively, forget context, cannot recover, or explore inefficiently.
Paper → This work adds planning, search, memory, reflection, world modeling, skill reuse, or context management.
Goal → Make the agent more reliable on long-horizon interactive tasks.
```

The first goal is to understand **what the paper changes inside the agent decision loop**.  
Only after that should it be connected to S5.3 and the thesis.

---

## Core idea

Connect step-by-step agents, tree-search agents, and full-plan-in-advance agents to planning paradigms, then evaluate trajectories with process-level metrics.

The paper mainly contributes to:

```text
planning taxonomy and trajectory metrics for web agents
```

This belongs to the agent planning layer:

```text
task instruction
→ current observation/state
→ planning or search
→ candidate actions
→ evaluation/reflection/correction
→ execution
→ memory/context update
```

---

## Key finding / main claim

The paper shows that different planning paradigms have different strengths: step-by-step agents align better with human trajectories, while full-plan-in-advance agents can achieve high element accuracy.

For S5.3, the important question is:

```text
How does the method improve decision-making beyond one-step ReAct-style action selection?
```

Typical S5.3 improvements include:

- planning ahead,
- exploring multiple branches,
- reflecting on failure,
- backtracking after mistakes,
- using memory and replay,
- summarizing long contexts,
- learning reusable skills,
- simulating action consequences,
- and allocating interaction budget more intelligently.

---

## Key evidence to extract from the paper

When reading this paper, extract these concrete evidence points:

- **Taxonomy mapping Step-by-Step to breadth-oriented traversal, Tree Search to best-first search, and Full-Plan-in-Advance to depth-oriented traversal.**
- **794 human-labeled WebArena trajectories.**
- **Five metrics: Recovery Rate, Repetitiveness Rate, Step Success Rate, Partial Success Rate, and Element Accuracy Rate.**

Also extract, if available:

- planning/search algorithm,
- memory representation,
- action space,
- benchmark and task horizon,
- success-rate improvement,
- compute/cost trade-off,
- ablation study,
- and failure modes.

---

## Limitations

- **Limitation 1:** It is an arXiv manuscript submitted to ACM, not confirmed accepted.
- **Limitation 2:** It focuses on planning diagnosis and metrics rather than a new high-performing web agent.
- **Limitation 3:** LLM-as-judge metrics require validation and can introduce evaluator bias.

General thesis-level limitation:

```text
Better planning does not automatically mean generalized web automation is solved.
The agent still needs perception, grounding, execution, verification, recovery, and safety.
```

Therefore, this paper should be used as **P2 support**, not as the only foundation for S5.3.
